## Summary: Semantic Release Report Display Architecture

I've thoroughly analyzed the codebase to understand how semantic release reports are displayed on the frontend. Here are my findings:

### 1. React Components (Frontend)

**Primary Display Components:**

- **`/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/web/components/semantic-releases/semantic-release-detail.tsx`** (605 lines)
  - Main detail view for individual semantic releases
  - Displays comprehensive report information with multiple sections:
    - Version heading with project name byline
    - Subject line from report
    - Status badge with timestamp
    - Risk & Urgency banner (with color-coded risk levels: CRITICAL/HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW)
    - Availability & Downloads section (status checks, download links, download commands)
    - Adoption narrative section
    - Source Releases table (provider, repository, version, date)
    - Context Sources section with metadata
    - Changelog Summary prose

- **`/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/web/components/semantic-releases/semantic-releases-list.tsx`** (354 lines)
  - Paginated list view of all semantic releases
  - Displays in table format with columns: Project, Version, Status, Urgency, Age
  - Includes project filter dropdown
  - Shows urgency as color-coded chips (Critical/High/Medium/Low)
  - Pagination support (15 items per page)
  - Delete functionality with confirmation dialog

**Page Routes:**
- `/web/app/semantic-releases/page.tsx` - Lists all semantic releases
- `/web/app/projects/[id]/semantic-releases/[srId]/page.tsx` - Detailed view of single release

### 2. Data Type Definitions

**TypeScript Types** (`/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/web/lib/api/types.ts`):

```typescript
interface SemanticRelease {
  id: string;
  project_id: string;
  project_name?: string;
  version: string;
  report?: SemanticReport;
  status: string;
  error?: string;
  created_at: string;
  completed_at?: string;
}

interface SemanticReport {
  subject?: string;
  risk_level?: string;
  risk_reason?: string;
  status_checks?: string[];
  changelog_summary?: string;
  download_commands?: string[];
  download_links?: string[];
  summary?: string;
  availability?: string;
  adoption?: string;
  urgency?: string;
  recommendation?: string;
}
```

### 3. API Endpoints

**Frontend API Client** (`/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/web/lib/api/client.ts`):

```typescript
export const semanticReleases = {
  listAll: (page = 1, perPage = 25) 
    → GET /semantic-releases?page={page}&per_page={perPage}
  
  list: (projectId, page = 1, perPage = 25) 
    → GET /projects/{projectId}/semantic-releases?page={page}&per_page={perPage}
  
  get: (id: string) 
    → GET /semantic-releases/{id}
  
  delete: (id: string) 
    → DELETE /semantic-releases/{id}
  
  getSources: (id: string) 
    → GET /semantic-releases/{id}/sources
};
```

**Go Backend Handlers** (`/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/api/semantic_releases.go`):

- `ListAll()` - GET /semantic-releases
- `List()` - GET /projects/{projectId}/semantic-releases
- `Get()` - GET /semantic-releases/{id}
- `ListSources()` - GET /semantic-releases/{id}/sources
- `Delete()` - DELETE /semantic-releases/{id}

### 4. Display Logic & Formatting

**Key Display Features:**

1. **Risk Level Visualization** (`getRiskColors()` function):
   - CRITICAL: Red (#dc2626) background with white text
   - HIGH: Orange (#d97706) background
   - MEDIUM: Yellow (#ca8a04) background
   - LOW: Green (#16a34a) background

2. **Download Link Parsing** (`getDownloadLabel()` function):
   - Detects platform-specific binaries from URLs (Linux x64, macOS ARM64, Windows x86, etc.)
   - Distinguishes direct downloads (dark button) from generic links (light pill-style)
   - Intelligently extracts platform info from filename and URL patterns

3. **Status Check Display**:
   - Rendered as green pills with checkmark icon
   - Indicates availability/readiness

4. **Download Command Display**:
   - Rendered in monospace font within bordered boxes
   - Copy-to-clipboard button on hover

5. **Source Releases Table**:
   - Links to external provider URLs (GitHub tags, Docker Hub tags, ECR Gallery)
   - Shows provider badge, repository, version chip, and relative time

6. **Urgency Chips** (in list view):
   - Color-coded: Critical (#dc2626), High (#f97316), Medium (#f59e0b), Low (#6b7280)
   - Rendered as filled rounded pills

### 5. Data Flow

**Data retrieval chain:**
1. Frontend calls `srApi.get(srId)` → POST /api/v1/semantic-releases/{id}
2. Backend returns `SemanticRelease` with embedded JSON `Report` field
3. Frontend uses `report` property to render display sections
4. Simultaneously fetches: sources, projects, context sources for additional context
5. All use SWR for client-side caching and revalidation

### 6. Integration Points

**Related entities displayed with semantic releases:**
- **Projects**: Name displayed as byline
- **Source Releases**: Listed in table below report
- **Context Sources**: Knowledge base/documentation links
- **Agent Runs**: Referenced in project agent configuration page

The architecture follows a server-rendered Next.js pattern with client-side hydration, using SWR for data fetching and Next.js Link for navigation. All styling uses Tailwind CSS with custom inline styles for branded color schemes (DM Sans and Fraunces fonts).

## Summary: Notification System Architecture

I've thoroughly explored the notification formatting and sending system in this codebase. Here's what I found:

### **High-Level Flow**

1. **Trigger**: When a new release is detected, a `NotifyJobArgs` job is enqueued in the River job queue (`/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/queue/jobs.go`)
2. **Worker**: The `NotifyWorker` processes each job, looking up the release and all source-level subscriptions (`/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/routing/worker.go`)
3. **Dispatch**: For each subscription, the worker sends notifications via the appropriate channel sender
4. **Agent Trigger**: After notifications, it checks agent rules to auto-trigger semantic release analysis

### **Core Files**

**Notification Models & Interfaces:**
- `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/routing/sender.go` - Base `Notification` struct and `Sender` interface
- `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/models/channel.go` - `NotificationChannel` model storing channel config
- `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/models/release.go` - `Release` model with enriched fields
- `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/models/semantic_release.go` - `SemanticReport` struct with SRE-focused fields

**Notification Channels (Senders):**
- `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/routing/slack.go` - Slack Block Kit formatting
- `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/routing/discord.go` - Discord embed formatting
- `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/routing/webhook.go` - Generic JSON webhook
- **No email/Telegram support** - Only these three channels are implemented

**Worker & Routing:**
- `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/routing/worker.go` - Main notification processor
- `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/routing/rules.go` - Agent rule evaluation logic

### **Notification Structure**

The base `Notification` struct (sender.go, lines 14-23):
```go
type Notification struct {
    Title       string // "ProjectName — New release: v1.0.0"
    Body        string // Raw JSON release data or SemanticReport JSON
    Version     string
    ProjectName string
    Provider    string // "github", "dockerhub", "ecr-public", "gitlab"
    Repository  string
    ReleaseURL  string // Internal Changelogue link
    SourceURL   string // Upstream provider link
}
```

### **Formatting by Channel**

**Slack Formatting** (slack.go, lines 56-178):
- Two paths: If body is a `SemanticReport`, uses `buildSemanticBlocks()` for rich formatting with risk emojis, status checks, changelog, download commands
- Fallback: Extracts changelog/release_url fields from raw JSON
- Uses Slack Block Kit API with headers, sections, dividers, context blocks
- Footer includes provider info and links

**Discord Formatting** (discord.go, lines 66-150):
- Two paths: If body is a `SemanticReport`, uses `buildSemanticEmbed()` with color coding based on risk level
- Fallback: Extracts known fields from raw JSON
- Uses Discord embed format with title, description, fields, footer
- Color coding: CRITICAL (0x111113), HIGH (0xDC2626), MEDIUM (0xD97706), LOW (0x16A34A)
- Description truncated to 4096 chars (Discord limit)
- Fields include Risk Level, Urgency, Availability, Adoption

**Webhook Formatting** (webhook.go, lines 34-56):
- If body is a `SemanticReport`, sends structured `webhookSemanticPayload` with report as field
- Fallback: Sends raw `Notification` struct as JSON
- Receives at configured webhook URL via POST

**Helper Functions** (sender.go):
- `ProviderURL()` - Builds upstream provider URLs
- `ProviderLabel()` - Readable names for providers
- `parseRawBody()` - Extracts changelog and release_url from raw JSON

### **SemanticReport Structure** (semantic_release.go)

When an agent run completes, it produces a `SemanticReport` with:
- `Subject` - Release summary
- `RiskLevel` - "LOW", "MEDIUM", "HIGH", "CRITICAL"
- `RiskReason` - Risk assessment details
- `StatusChecks` - Array of status check items
- `ChangelogSummary` - Summarized changelog
- `DownloadCommands` - Installation/download commands
- `DownloadLinks` - Download URLs
- `Availability`, `Adoption`, `Urgency`, `Recommendation`

### **Subscription & Filtering** (worker.go, lines 59-94)

- Version filters support inclusive regex patterns
- Can exclude pre-releases
- Source-level subscriptions route to channels

### **Agent Rule Triggering** (worker.go, lines 149-198)

After sending notifications, auto-triggers agent if:
- `OnMajorRelease` - New major version detected
- `OnMinorRelease` - New minor version detected  
- `OnSecurityPatch` - Version contains "security", "cve", or "vuln"
- `VersionPattern` - Custom regex pattern matches

### **Key Findings**

1. **Semantic-aware formatting** - All channel senders detect and richly format `SemanticReport` bodies differently from raw release data
2. **3 supported channels** - Webhook, Slack, Discord (no email/Telegram)
3. **Extensible pattern** - New channels can be added by implementing the `Sender` interface
4. **Rich metadata** - Notifications include project name, provider, repository, internal/external links
5. **Risk-based styling** - Slack uses emojis, Discord uses color coding based on risk level
6. **Graceful fallback** - If body isn't a semantic report, extracts readable fields from raw JSON
7. **URL generation** - Builds provider-specific links (GitHub releases, Docker Hub tags, ECR, GitLab)

All code is in `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/routing/` directory with comprehensive test coverage in `*_test.go` files.

## Comprehensive Analysis: Semantic Release Report Generation in Changelogue

I've completed a thorough exploration of the semantic release report generation system. Here's the complete breakdown:

---

### 1. **Data Model & Structures**

**Core Model:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/models/semantic_release.go`

```go
type SemanticReport struct {
    // SRE-focused fields
    Subject          string   // e.g., "Ready to Deploy: Geth v1.10.15 (Critical Update)"
    RiskLevel        string   // CRITICAL|HIGH|MEDIUM|LOW
    RiskReason       string   // e.g., "Hard Fork detected in Discord #announcements"
    StatusChecks     []string // e.g., ["Docker Image Verified", "Binaries Available"]
    ChangelogSummary string   // One-line changelog summary
    DownloadCommands []string // e.g., ["docker pull ethereum/client-go:v1.10.15"]
    DownloadLinks    []string // Direct artifact URLs + release page URLs
    
    // Existing fields (backward compatible)
    Summary        string   // Fallback summary if parsing fails
    Availability   string   // GA|RC|Beta
    Adoption       string   // Percentage or recommendation
    Urgency        string   // Critical|High|Medium|Low
    Recommendation string   // Actionable 1-2 sentence recommendation
}

type SemanticRelease struct {
    ID          string          // UUID
    ProjectID   string          // FK to projects
    ProjectName string          // Denormalized for display
    Version     string          // e.g., "v1.10.15"
    Report      json.RawMessage // Serialized SemanticReport JSON
    Status      string          // completed|pending|failed
    Error       string          // Error message if failed
    CreatedAt   time.Time
    CompletedAt *time.Time
}
```

**Database Schema:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/db/migrations.go`

```sql
CREATE TABLE semantic_releases (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    project_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES projects(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    version VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    report JSONB,
    status VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'pending',
    error TEXT,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    completed_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
    UNIQUE(project_id, version)
);

-- Join table: which source releases compose a semantic release
CREATE TABLE semantic_release_sources (
    semantic_release_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES semantic_releases(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    release_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES releases(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    PRIMARY KEY (semantic_release_id, release_id)
);
```

---

### 2. **Agent/LLM Code - Report Generation Pipeline**

**Core Orchestrator:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/orchestrator.go`

**Architecture:**
- Uses Google's **ADK-Go (Agent Development Kit for Go) v0.5.0** with Gemini or OpenAI models
- Three-tier agent structure:
  1. **Data Sub-Agent** - queries releases and context sources from database
  2. **Search Sub-Agent** - performs web search for external context (Gemini Google Search or OpenAI web search)
  3. **Root Agent** ("release_analyst") - orchestrates both sub-agents and produces final JSON report

**Report Generation Flow:**

```
1. TriggerAgentRun() → Creates AgentRun in database
2. River queue → AgentWorker picks up job
3. RunAgent() lifecycle:
   a. Load project + agent configuration
   b. BuildAgent() with project-scoped tools
   c. executeAgent():
      - Create ADK-Go runner with logging plugin
      - Run agent with user prompt: "Analyze version X. Cross-check all sources and produce a semantic release report."
      - Capture final text response from LLM
      - parseReport() → Convert response JSON to SemanticReport struct
      - CreateSemanticRelease() with linked source releases
      - Send project-level notifications (Slack, Discord, webhooks)
   d. Mark run as completed/failed
```

**Prompt Template (DefaultInstruction):** Lines 46-76 in `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/orchestrator.go`

```
Focus ONLY on version {{VERSION}}. Cross-check across all sources.

CRITICAL: Your final response MUST be a single JSON object and nothing else.

The JSON object must have exactly these fields:
{
  "subject": "Ready to Deploy: <Project> <Version> (<Risk Summary>)",
  "risk_level": "CRITICAL|HIGH|MEDIUM|LOW",
  "risk_reason": "Why this risk level",
  "status_checks": ["Docker Image Verified"],
  "changelog_summary": "One-line summary of key changes",
  "availability": "GA|RC|Beta",
  "adoption": "Percentage or recommendation",
  "urgency": "Critical|High|Medium|Low",
  "recommendation": "Actionable 1-2 sentence recommendation",
  "download_commands": ["docker pull ..."],
  "download_links": ["https://...", "https://..."]
}
```

**Custom Prompts:** Projects can define custom `AgentPrompt` in the database (stored as TEXT in `projects.agent_prompt`), which gets prepended to `DefaultInstruction`.

**Projects can also define `AgentRules`:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/models/project.go`

```go
type AgentRules struct {
    OnMajorRelease    bool   // Trigger on major version bumps
    OnMinorRelease    bool   // Trigger on minor version bumps
    OnSecurityPatch   bool   // Trigger on security patches
    VersionPattern    string // Regex for custom version matching
    WaitForAllSources bool   // Wait for all sources to have the version before running agent
}
```

---

### 3. **Agent Tools**

**File:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/tools.go`

Three primary tools available to the agent:

1. **get_releases** - Fetch paginated releases for the project
   - Returns: version, raw_data (full upstream payload), timestamps
   
2. **get_release_detail** - Get detailed information for a specific release by ID
   - Returns: full raw data payload from upstream provider (changelog, commits, etc.)
   
3. **list_context_sources** - Query project context sources (runbooks, docs, dashboards)
   - Returns: project-scoped references for agent research

All tools are scoped by `projectID` to ensure isolation.

---

### 4. **Report Parsing & Fallback Logic**

**parseReport() function:** Lines 487-514 in orchestrator.go

- Strips markdown code fences (```` ``` ````) if agent mistakenly wraps JSON
- Unmarshals into `SemanticReport` struct
- **Fallback behavior:** If JSON parsing fails or missing `subject` and `summary`, stores raw text as `Summary` field
- **Backward compatibility:** Supports old format with just `summary` field

**Test Example:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/models/semantic_release_test.go`

```go
report := SemanticReport{
    Subject:          "🚀 Ready to Deploy: Geth v1.10.15 (Critical Update)",
    RiskLevel:        "CRITICAL",
    RiskReason:       "Hard Fork detected in Discord #announcements",
    StatusChecks:     []string{"Docker Image Verified", "Binaries Available"},
    ChangelogSummary: "Fixes sync bug in block 14,000,000",
    Availability:     "GA",
    Adoption:         "12% of network updated",
    Urgency:          "Critical",
    Recommendation:   "Wait for 25% adoption unless urgent.",
    DownloadCommands: []string{"docker pull ethereum/client-go:v1.10.15"},
    DownloadLinks:    []string{"https://github.com/ethereum/go-ethereum/releases/tag/v1.10.15"},
}
```

---

### 5. **Persistence Layer**

**File:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/api/pgstore.go`

**Key Operations:**

```go
// CreateSemanticRelease inserts a semantic release + source links in a transaction
func (s *PgStore) CreateSemanticRelease(ctx context.Context, sr *models.SemanticRelease, releaseIDs []string) error {
    // Inserts into semantic_releases table
    // Inserts N rows into semantic_release_sources join table
}

// Report is stored as JSONB in the report column
// Queries can filter by report urgency:
// SELECT ... WHERE report->>'urgency' IN ('critical', 'high')
```

**Efficiency Features:**
- Join table (`semantic_release_sources`) links each semantic release to 1+ source releases
- Allows tracking which source releases contributed to a semantic release
- UNIQUE constraint on (project_id, version) prevents duplicate semantic releases

---

### 6. **Notification & Report Formatting**

**File:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/routing/webhook.go`

Reports are sent to subscribed notification channels (Slack, Discord, webhooks).

**Webhook Payload Structure:**
```go
type webhookSemanticPayload struct {
    Title       string                `json:"title"`
    Version     string                `json:"version"`
    ProjectName string                `json:"project_name,omitempty"`
    Provider    string                `json:"provider,omitempty"`
    Repository  string                `json:"repository,omitempty"`
    ReleaseURL  string                `json:"release_url,omitempty"`
    SourceURL   string                `json:"source_url,omitempty"`
    Report      *models.SemanticReport `json:"report"` // Full report as structured JSON
}
```

**Discord Embed Formatting:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/routing/discord.go`

Reports are rendered as rich Discord embeds:
- **Title:** Project + Version
- **Description:** Subject, Risk Reason, Status Checks, Changelog, Download Commands, Download Links
- **Fields:** Risk Level (with emoji), Urgency, Availability, Adoption
- **Color:** Determined by risk level (CRITICAL=black, HIGH=red, MEDIUM=orange, LOW=green)
- **Footer:** Provider + Repository info

---

### 7. **Agent Entrypoint (Development)**

**File:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/cmd/agent/main.go`

Standalone CLI for testing agents:
```bash
go run ./cmd/agent --project-id=<uuid> [web api webui]
```

Launches ADK-Web UI for interactive agent development & testing.

---

### 8. **API Endpoints**

**Semantic Releases API:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/API.md`

```
GET  /api/v1/semantic-releases              # List all semantic releases
GET  /api/v1/projects/{projectId}/semantic-releases  # List for project
GET  /api/v1/semantic-releases/{id}         # Get single release with full report
GET  /api/v1/semantic-releases/{id}/sources # List source releases that composed it
DELETE /api/v1/semantic-releases/{id}       # Delete a semantic release

POST /api/v1/projects/{projectId}/agent/run # Trigger agent run
GET  /api/v1/projects/{projectId}/agent/runs # List agent runs
GET  /api/v1/agent-runs/{id}                # Get single agent run details
```

**Response includes full `Report` field** as JSON-serialized `SemanticReport`.

---

### 9. **Key Files Summary**

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `/internal/models/semantic_release.go` | `SemanticReport`, `SemanticRelease` structs |
| `/internal/agent/orchestrator.go` | Main report generation pipeline, `BuildAgent()`, `RunAgent()`, `parseReport()`, `DefaultInstruction` |
| `/internal/agent/tools.go` | ADK function tools (get_releases, get_release_detail, list_context_sources) |
| `/internal/agent/worker.go` | River job handler that calls `orchestrator.RunAgent()` |
| `/internal/agent/model.go` | LLM provider abstraction (Gemini/OpenAI) |
| `/internal/api/pgstore.go` | `CreateSemanticRelease()`, `ListSemanticReleases()`, `GetSemanticRelease()` |
| `/internal/api/semantic_releases.go` | HTTP handlers for semantic releases endpoints |
| `/internal/routing/webhook.go` | Webhook payload formatting with `SemanticReport` |
| `/internal/routing/discord.go` | Discord embed rendering from `SemanticReport` |
| `/internal/db/migrations.go` | `semantic_releases` and `semantic_release_sources` table schemas |
| `/cmd/agent/main.go` | Standalone agent CLI for development |
| `/cmd/server/main.go` | Main server that orchestrates polling, agents, and API |

---

{
  "subject": "Ready to Deploy: Geth v1.10.15",
  "risk_level": "CRITICAL",
  "risk_reason": "Hard Fork detected",
  "status_checks": ["Docker Image Verified"],
  "changelog_summary": "Fixes sync bug",
  "availability": "GA",
  "adoption": "12% updated",
  "urgency": "Critical",
  "recommendation": "Wait for 25% adoption.",
  "download_commands": ["docker pull ethereum/client-go:v1.10.15"],
  "download_links": ["https://example.com/release"]
}
```

---

### Summary

The semantic release report generation is a **well-architected, agent-driven system** that:

1. **Uses ADK-Go with sub-agents** for structured reasoning (data lookup + web search)
2. **Enforces JSON output format** via prompt engineering + parsing validation
3. **Supports custom prompts per project** while maintaining baseline structure
4. **Provides rich context** via project-scoped tools (releases, context sources)
5. **Handles failures gracefully** with fallback to raw text storage
6. **Integrates seamlessly** with notification routing (webhooks, Slack, Discord)
7. **Maintains full audit trail** via AgentRun records and database transactions
8. **Supports multi-source correlation** via semantic_release_sources join table

All code is production-ready with comprehensive error handling, logging, and transactional consistency via PostgreSQL.

     1→package routing

     3→import (
     4→	"bytes"

    10→
    11→	"github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/models"
    12→)
    13→
    14→// discordEmbedDescriptionLimit is Discord's maximum embed description length.
    15→const discordEmbedDescriptionLimit = 4096
    16→
    17→type discordConfig struct {
    18→	WebhookURL string `json:"webhook_url"`
    19→}
    20→
    21→// DiscordSender sends notifications as Discord webhook messages with embeds.
    22→type DiscordSender struct {
    23→	Client *http.Client
    24→}
    25→
    26→// discordPayload is the Discord webhook payload.
    27→type discordPayload struct {
    28→	Embeds []discordEmbed `json:"embeds"`
    29→}
    30→
    31→type discordEmbed struct {
    32→	Title       string              `json:"title"`
    33→	URL         string              `json:"url,omitempty"`
    34→	Description string              `json:"description"`
    35→	Color       int                 `json:"color"`
    36→	Fields      []discordEmbedField `json:"fields,omitempty"`
    37→	Footer      *discordEmbedFooter `json:"footer,omitempty"`
    38→}
    39→
    40→type discordEmbedField struct {
    41→	Name   string `json:"name"`
    42→	Value  string `json:"value"`
    43→	Inline bool   `json:"inline"`
    44→}
    45→
    46→type discordEmbedFooter struct {
    47→	Text string `json:"text"`
    48→}
    49→
    50→// discordRiskColor returns a Discord embed color based on risk level.
    51→func discordRiskColor(level string) int {
    52→	switch strings.ToUpper(strings.TrimSpace(level)) {
    53→	case "CRITICAL":
    54→		return 0x111113 // near-black
    55→	case "HIGH":
    56→		return 0xDC2626 // red
    57→	case "MEDIUM":
    58→		return 0xD97706 // amber
    59→	case "LOW":
    60→		return 0x16A34A // green
    61→	default:
    62→		return 0x5865F2 // Discord blurple
    63→	}
    64→}
    65→
    66→// buildSemanticEmbed builds a rich Discord embed from a SemanticReport.
    67→func buildSemanticEmbed(title string, version string, report *models.SemanticReport, msg Notification) discordEmbed {
    68→	var descParts []string
    69→
    70→	if report.Subject != "" {
    71→		descParts = append(descParts, fmt.Sprintf("**%s**", report.Subject))
    72→	}
    73→
    74→	if report.RiskReason != "" {
    75→		descParts = append(descParts, fmt.Sprintf(">>> %s", report.RiskReason))
    76→	}
    77→
    78→	if len(report.StatusChecks) > 0 {
    79→		var checks []string
    80→		for _, check := range report.StatusChecks {
    81→			checks = append(checks, fmt.Sprintf("✅ %s", check))
    82→		}
    83→		descParts = append(descParts, strings.Join(checks, "\n"))
    84→	}
    85→
    86→	summary := report.ChangelogSummary
    87→	if summary == "" {
    88→		summary = report.Summary
    89→	}
    90→	if summary != "" {
    91→		descParts = append(descParts, fmt.Sprintf("**Changelog**\n%s", summary))
    92→	}
    93→
    94→	if len(report.DownloadCommands) > 0 {
    95→		descParts = append(descParts, fmt.Sprintf("**Download Commands**\n```\n%s\n```", strings.Join(report.DownloadCommands, "\n")))
    96→	}
    97→
    98→	if len(report.DownloadLinks) > 0 {
    99→		var links []string
   100→		for _, link := range report.DownloadLinks {
   101→			links = append(links, fmt.Sprintf("• %s", link))
   102→		}
   103→		descParts = append(descParts, fmt.Sprintf("**Download Links**\n%s", strings.Join(links, "\n")))
   104→	}
   105→
   106→	// Add links section
   107→	var linkParts []string
   108→	if msg.SourceURL != "" {
   109→		linkParts = append(linkParts, fmt.Sprintf("[View on %s](%s)", ProviderLabel(msg.Provider), msg.SourceURL))
   110→	}
   111→	if msg.ReleaseURL != "" {
   112→		linkParts = append(linkParts, fmt.Sprintf("[View in Changelogue](%s)", msg.ReleaseURL))
   113→	}
   114→	if len(linkParts) > 0 {
   115→		descParts = append(descParts, strings.Join(linkParts, "  •  "))
   116→	}
   117→
   118→	description := strings.Join(descParts, "\n\n")
   119→	if len(description) > discordEmbedDescriptionLimit {
   120→		description = description[:discordEmbedDescriptionLimit-3] + "..."
   121→	}
   122→
   123→	fields := []discordEmbedField{
   124→		{Name: "Risk Level", Value: fmt.Sprintf("%s %s", riskEmoji(report.RiskLevel), report.RiskLevel), Inline: true},
   125→		{Name: "Urgency", Value: report.Urgency, Inline: true},
   126→	}
   127→	if report.Availability != "" {
   128→		fields = append(fields, discordEmbedField{Name: "Availability", Value: report.Availability, Inline: true})
   129→	}
   130→	if report.Adoption != "" {
   131→		fields = append(fields, discordEmbedField{Name: "Adoption", Value: report.Adoption, Inline: false})
   132→	}
   133→
   134→	// Footer with source info
   135→	var footerText string
   136→	if msg.Provider != "" && msg.Repository != "" {
   137→		footerText = fmt.Sprintf("%s · %s", ProviderLabel(msg.Provider), msg.Repository)
   138→	}
   139→
   140→	embed := discordEmbed{
   141→		Title:       title,
   142→		Description: description,
   143→		Color:       discordRiskColor(report.RiskLevel),
   144→		Fields:      fields,
   145→	}
   146→	if footerText != "" {
   147→		embed.Footer = &discordEmbedFooter{Text: footerText}
   148→	}
   149→	return embed
   150→}
   151→
   152→func (s *DiscordSender) Send(ctx context.Context, ch *models.NotificationChannel, msg Notification) error {
   153→	var cfg discordConfig
   154→	if err := json.Unmarshal(ch.Config, &cfg); err != nil {
   155→		return fmt.Errorf("parse discord config: %w", err)
   156→	}
   157→
   158→	var embed discordEmbed
   159→	var report models.SemanticReport
   160→	if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(msg.Body), &report); err == nil && report.Subject != "" {
   161→		embed = buildSemanticEmbed(msg.Title, msg.Version, &report, msg)
   162→	} else {
   163→		// Fallback: extract known fields from raw data for a readable message.
   164→		var descParts []string
   165→		if fields, ok := parseRawBody(msg.Body); ok {
   166→			if fields.Changelog != "" {
   167→				descParts = append(descParts, fields.Changelog)
   168→			}
   169→		} else {
   170→			descParts = append(descParts, msg.Body)
   171→		}
   172→
   173→		// Add links
   174→		var linkParts []string
   175→		if msg.SourceURL != "" {
   176→			linkParts = append(linkParts, fmt.Sprintf("[View on %s](%s)", ProviderLabel(msg.Provider), msg.SourceURL))
   177→		}
   178→		if msg.ReleaseURL != "" {
   179→			linkParts = append(linkParts, fmt.Sprintf("[View in Changelogue](%s)", msg.ReleaseURL))
   180→		}
   181→		if len(linkParts) > 0 {
   182→			descParts = append(descParts, strings.Join(linkParts, "  •  "))
   183→		}
   184→
   185→		description := strings.Join(descParts, "\n\n")
   186→		if len(description) > discordEmbedDescriptionLimit {
   187→			description = description[:discordEmbedDescriptionLimit-3] + "..."
   188→		}
   189→
   190→		embed = discordEmbed{
   191→			Title:       msg.Title,
   192→			Description: description,
   193→			Color:       0x5865F2,
   194→			Fields: []discordEmbedField{
   195→				{Name: "Version", Value: msg.Version, Inline: true},
   196→			},
   197→		}
   198→		if msg.Provider != "" && msg.Repository != "" {
   199→			embed.Footer = &discordEmbedFooter{Text: fmt.Sprintf("%s · %s", ProviderLabel(msg.Provider), msg.Repository)}
   200→		}
   201→	}
   202→
   203→	payload := discordPayload{Embeds: []discordEmbed{embed}}
   204→
   205→	body, err := json.Marshal(payload)
   206→	if err != nil {
   207→		return fmt.Errorf("marshal discord payload: %w", err)
   208→	}
   209→
   210→	req, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodPost, cfg.WebhookURL, bytes.NewReader(body))
   211→	if err != nil {
   212→		return fmt.Errorf("create request: %w", err)
   213→	}
   214→	req.Header.Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
   215→
   216→	client := s.Client
   217→	if client == nil {
   218→		client = http.DefaultClient
   219→	}
   220→
   221→	resp, err := client.Do(req)
   222→	if err != nil {
   223→		return fmt.Errorf("send discord notification: %w", err)
   224→	}
   225→	defer resp.Body.Close()
   226→
   227→	if resp.StatusCode >= 400 {
   228→		return fmt.Errorf("discord returned status %d", resp.StatusCode)
   229→	}
   230→	return nil
   231→}

     1→package routing
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"bytes"

    11→	"github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/models"

    13→
    14→type slackConfig struct {
    15→	WebhookURL string `json:"webhook_url"`
    16→}
    17→
    18→// SlackSender sends notifications as Slack Block Kit messages via incoming webhook.
    19→type SlackSender struct {
    20→	Client *http.Client
    21→}
    22→
    23→// slackPayload is the Slack incoming webhook payload using Block Kit.
    24→type slackPayload struct {
    25→	Blocks []slackBlock `json:"blocks"`
    26→}
    27→
    28→type slackBlock struct {
    29→	Type     string      `json:"type"`
    30→	Text     *slackText  `json:"text,omitempty"`
    31→	Fields   []slackText `json:"fields,omitempty"`
    32→	Elements []slackText `json:"elements,omitempty"`
    33→}
    34→
    35→type slackText struct {
    36→	Type string `json:"type"`
    37→	Text string `json:"text"`
    38→}
    39→
    40→// riskEmoji returns an emoji indicator for the given risk level.
    41→func riskEmoji(level string) string {
    42→	switch strings.ToUpper(strings.TrimSpace(level)) {
    43→	case "LOW":
    44→		return "🟢"
    45→	case "MEDIUM":
    46→		return "🟡"
    47→	case "HIGH":
    48→		return "🔴"
    49→	case "CRITICAL":
    50→		return "⚫"
    51→	default:
    52→		return "⚪"
    53→	}
    54→}
    55→
    56→// buildSemanticBlocks builds rich Slack Block Kit blocks from a SemanticReport.
    57→func buildSemanticBlocks(title string, report *models.SemanticReport, msg Notification) []slackBlock {
    58→	blocks := []slackBlock{
    59→		{Type: "header", Text: &slackText{Type: "plain_text", Text: title}},
    60→	}
    61→
    62→	// Subject as the lead section
    63→	if report.Subject != "" {
    64→		blocks = append(blocks, slackBlock{
    65→			Type: "section",
    66→			Text: &slackText{Type: "mrkdwn", Text: fmt.Sprintf("*%s*", report.Subject)},
    67→		})
    68→	}
    69→
    70→	// Risk + Urgency + Availability as compact fields
    71→	blocks = append(blocks, slackBlock{
    72→		Type: "section",
    73→		Fields: []slackText{
    74→			{Type: "mrkdwn", Text: fmt.Sprintf("*Risk Level:*\n%s %s", riskEmoji(report.RiskLevel), report.RiskLevel)},
    75→			{Type: "mrkdwn", Text: fmt.Sprintf("*Urgency:*\n%s", report.Urgency)},
    76→			{Type: "mrkdwn", Text: fmt.Sprintf("*Availability:*\n%s", report.Availability)},
    77→		},
    78→	})
    79→
    80→	// Risk reason
    81→	if report.RiskReason != "" {
    82→		blocks = append(blocks,
    83→			slackBlock{Type: "divider"},
    84→			slackBlock{
    85→				Type: "section",
    86→				Text: &slackText{Type: "mrkdwn", Text: fmt.Sprintf("*Risk Assessment*\n%s", report.RiskReason)},
    87→			},
    88→		)
    89→	}
    90→
    91→	// Status checks
    92→	if len(report.StatusChecks) > 0 {
    93→		var items []string
    94→		for _, check := range report.StatusChecks {
    95→			items = append(items, fmt.Sprintf("• %s", check))
    96→		}
    97→		blocks = append(blocks,
    98→			slackBlock{Type: "divider"},
    99→			slackBlock{
   100→				Type: "section",
   101→				Text: &slackText{Type: "mrkdwn", Text: fmt.Sprintf("*Status Checks*\n%s", strings.Join(items, "\n"))},
   102→			},
   103→		)
   104→	}
   105→
   106→	// Changelog summary
   107→	summary := report.ChangelogSummary
   108→	if summary == "" {
   109→		summary = report.Summary
   110→	}
   111→	if summary != "" {
   112→		blocks = append(blocks,
   113→			slackBlock{Type: "divider"},
   114→			slackBlock{
   115→				Type: "section",
   116→				Text: &slackText{Type: "mrkdwn", Text: fmt.Sprintf("*Changelog*\n%s", summary)},
   117→			},
   118→		)
   119→	}
   120→
   121→	// Adoption
   122→	if report.Adoption != "" {
   123→		blocks = append(blocks, slackBlock{
   124→			Type: "section",
   125→			Text: &slackText{Type: "mrkdwn", Text: fmt.Sprintf("*Adoption*\n%s", report.Adoption)},
   126→		})
   127→	}
   128→
   129→	// Download commands as code block
   130→	if len(report.DownloadCommands) > 0 {
   131→		blocks = append(blocks,
   132→			slackBlock{Type: "divider"},
   133→			slackBlock{
   134→				Type: "section",
   135→				Text: &slackText{Type: "mrkdwn", Text: fmt.Sprintf("*Download Commands*\n```%s```", strings.Join(report.DownloadCommands, "\n"))},
   136→			},
   137→		)
   138→	}
   139→
   140→	// Download links
   141→	if len(report.DownloadLinks) > 0 {
   142→		var links []string
   143→		for _, link := range report.DownloadLinks {
   144→			links = append(links, fmt.Sprintf("• <%s>", link))
   145→		}
   146→		blocks = append(blocks, slackBlock{
   147→			Type: "section",
   148→			Text: &slackText{Type: "mrkdwn", Text: fmt.Sprintf("*Download Links*\n%s", strings.Join(links, "\n"))},
   149→		})
   150→	}
   151→
   152→	// Footer context with source info and links
   153→	var footerParts []string
   154→	if msg.Provider != "" && msg.Repository != "" {
   155→		footerParts = append(footerParts, fmt.Sprintf("%s · %s", ProviderLabel(msg.Provider), msg.Repository))
   156→	}
   157→	if msg.SourceURL != "" {
   158→		footerParts = append(footerParts, fmt.Sprintf("<%s|View on %s>", msg.SourceURL, ProviderLabel(msg.Provider)))
   159→	}
   160→	if msg.ReleaseURL != "" {
   161→		footerParts = append(footerParts, fmt.Sprintf("<%s|View in Changelogue>", msg.ReleaseURL))
   162→	}
   163→	footerText := "📡 _Sent by Changelogue_"
   164→	if len(footerParts) > 0 {
   165→		footerText = strings.Join(footerParts, "  |  ") + "\n" + footerText
   166→	}
   167→	blocks = append(blocks,
   168→		slackBlock{Type: "divider"},
   169→		slackBlock{
   170→			Type: "context",
   171→			Elements: []slackText{
   172→				{Type: "mrkdwn", Text: footerText},
   173→			},
   174→		},
   175→	)
   176→
   177→	return blocks
   178→}
   179→
   180→func (s *SlackSender) Send(ctx context.Context, ch *models.NotificationChannel, msg Notification) error {
   181→	var cfg slackConfig
   182→	if err := json.Unmarshal(ch.Config, &cfg); err != nil {
   183→		return fmt.Errorf("parse slack config: %w", err)
   184→	}
   185→
   186→	// Try to parse body as a SemanticReport for rich formatting.
   187→	var blocks []slackBlock
   188→	var report models.SemanticReport
   189→	if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(msg.Body), &report); err == nil && report.Subject != "" {
   190→		blocks = buildSemanticBlocks(msg.Title, &report, msg)
   191→	} else {
   192→		// Fallback: extract known fields from raw data for a readable message.
   193→		blocks = []slackBlock{
   194→			{Type: "header", Text: &slackText{Type: "plain_text", Text: msg.Title}},
   195→		}
   196→		if fields, ok := parseRawBody(msg.Body); ok {
   197→			if fields.Changelog != "" {
   198→				blocks = append(blocks, slackBlock{
   199→					Type: "section",
   200→					Text: &slackText{Type: "mrkdwn", Text: fields.Changelog},
   201→				})
   202→			}
   203→		} else {
   204→			blocks = append(blocks, slackBlock{
   205→				Type: "section",
   206→				Text: &slackText{Type: "mrkdwn", Text: msg.Body},
   207→			})
   208→		}
   209→
   210→		// Add source info and links
   211→		var linkParts []string
   212→		if msg.Provider != "" && msg.Repository != "" {
   213→			linkParts = append(linkParts, fmt.Sprintf("%s · %s", ProviderLabel(msg.Provider), msg.Repository))
   214→		}
   215→		if msg.SourceURL != "" {
   216→			linkParts = append(linkParts, fmt.Sprintf("<%s|View on %s>", msg.SourceURL, ProviderLabel(msg.Provider)))
   217→		}
   218→		if msg.ReleaseURL != "" {
   219→			linkParts = append(linkParts, fmt.Sprintf("<%s|View in Changelogue>", msg.ReleaseURL))
   220→		}
   221→		if len(linkParts) > 0 {
   222→			blocks = append(blocks, slackBlock{
   223→				Type: "context",
   224→				Elements: []slackText{
   225→					{Type: "mrkdwn", Text: strings.Join(linkParts, "  |  ")},
   226→				},
   227→			})
   228→		}
   229→	}
   230→
   231→	payload := slackPayload{Blocks: blocks}
   232→
   233→	body, err := json.Marshal(payload)
   234→	if err != nil {
   235→		return fmt.Errorf("marshal slack payload: %w", err)
   236→	}
   237→
   238→	req, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodPost, cfg.WebhookURL, bytes.NewReader(body))
   239→	if err != nil {
   240→		return fmt.Errorf("create request: %w", err)
   241→	}
   242→	req.Header.Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
   243→
   244→	client := s.Client
   245→	if client == nil {
   246→		client = http.DefaultClient
   247→	}
   248→
   249→	resp, err := client.Do(req)
   250→	if err != nil {
   251→		return fmt.Errorf("send slack notification: %w", err)
   252→	}
   253→	defer resp.Body.Close()
   254→
   255→	if resp.StatusCode >= 400 {
   256→		return fmt.Errorf("slack returned status %d", resp.StatusCode)
   257→	}
   258→	return nil
   259→}
   260→

    30→// OrchestratorStore defines all data access methods required by the agent
    31→// orchestrator to load project configuration, run the agent, and persist
    32→// the resulting semantic release.
    33→type OrchestratorStore interface {
    34→	AgentDataStore
    35→	GetProject(ctx context.Context, id string) (*models.Project, error)
    36→	GetAgentRun(ctx context.Context, id string) (*models.AgentRun, error)
    37→	UpdateAgentRunStatus(ctx context.Context, id, status string) error
    38→	CreateSemanticRelease(ctx context.Context, sr *models.SemanticRelease, releaseIDs []string) error
    39→	UpdateAgentRunResult(ctx context.Context, id string, semanticReleaseID string) error
    40→	ListProjectSubscriptions(ctx context.Context, projectID string) ([]models.Subscription, error)
    41→	GetChannel(ctx context.Context, id string) (*models.NotificationChannel, error)
    42→	ListSourcesByProject(ctx context.Context, projectID string, page, perPage int) ([]models.Source, int, error)
    43→	HasReleaseForVersion(ctx context.Context, sourceID, version string) (bool, error)
    44→}
    45→
    46→const DefaultInstruction = `You are a release intelligence agent analyzing version {{VERSION}} of a software project.
    47→
    48→Focus ONLY on version {{VERSION}}. Cross-check this version across all available sources.
    49→
    50→Use the available tools to:
    51→1. Fetch releases and find the one matching {{VERSION}} from each source.
    52→2. Inspect release details (changelogs, commit data, raw payloads) for {{VERSION}} only.
    53→3. Check binary/image availability directly from the source data.
    54→4. Review the project's context sources (runbooks, documentation) for relevant background.
    55→5. Use web search ONLY when you need additional context not available from sources
    56→   (e.g., community sentiment, security advisories, network adoption stats, known issues).
    57→
    58→CRITICAL: Your final response MUST be a single JSON object and nothing else.
    59→Do not include any explanation, commentary, or markdown formatting — just the raw JSON.
    60→
    61→For download_links: prefer direct binary/artifact URLs for specific platforms (e.g., linux-amd64, darwin-arm64, windows-amd64 .tar.gz/.zip files) over generic release pages. Include the general release page URL as well, but prioritize direct download links when available.
    62→
    63→The JSON object must have exactly these fields:
    64→{
    65→  "subject": "Ready to Deploy: <Project> <Version> (<Risk Summary>)",
    66→  "risk_level": "CRITICAL|HIGH|MEDIUM|LOW",
    67→  "risk_reason": "Why this risk level (e.g., 'Hard Fork detected in Discord #announcements')",
    68→  "status_checks": ["Docker Image Verified", "Binaries Available"],
    69→  "changelog_summary": "One-line summary of key changes (e.g., 'Fixes sync bug in block 14,000,000')",
    70→  "availability": "GA|RC|Beta",
    71→  "adoption": "Percentage or recommendation (e.g., '12% of network updated (Wait recommended if not urgent)')",
    72→  "urgency": "Critical|High|Medium|Low",
    73→  "recommendation": "Actionable 1-2 sentence recommendation for the SRE team",
    74→  "download_commands": ["docker pull ethereum/client-go:v1.10.15"],
    75→  "download_links": ["https://gethstore.blob.core.windows.net/builds/geth-linux-amd64-1.10.15-8be800ff.tar.gz", "https://gethstore.blob.core.windows.net/builds/geth-darwin-arm64-1.10.15-8be800ff.tar.gz", "https://github.com/ethereum/go-ethereum/releases/tag/v1.10.15"]
    76→}`
    77→
    78→// BuildAgent creates the ADK-Go LLM agent for a given project. This is used
    79→// by both the production orchestrator and the dev entrypoint to ensure the
    80→// same agent configuration is used everywhere.

     1→"use client";
     2→
     3→import useSWR from "swr";
     4→import Link from "next/link";
     5→import {
     6→  semanticReleases as srApi,
     7→  projects as projectsApi,
     8→  sources as sourcesApi,
     9→  contextSources,
    10→} from "@/lib/api/client";
    11→import { useRouter } from "next/navigation";
    12→import { StatusDot } from "@/components/ui/status-dot";
    13→import { VersionChip } from "@/components/ui/version-chip";
    14→import { SectionLabel } from "@/components/ui/section-label";
    15→import { ProviderBadge } from "@/components/ui/provider-badge";
    16→import { timeAgo } from "@/lib/format";
    17→import { getPathSegment } from "@/lib/path";
    18→import {
    19→  ArrowLeft,
    20→  Check,
    21→  ExternalLink,
    22→  Copy,
    23→  ShieldAlert,
    24→  BookOpen,
    25→  Download,
    26→} from "lucide-react";
    27→
    28→function getRiskColors(riskLevel?: string) {
    29→  switch (riskLevel?.toUpperCase()) {
    30→    case "CRITICAL":
    31→      return { border: "#dc2626", bg: "#fff1f2", text: "#991b1b" };
    32→    case "HIGH":
    33→      return { border: "#d97706", bg: "#fff8f0", text: "#92400e" };
    34→    case "MEDIUM":
    35→      return { border: "#ca8a04", bg: "#fefce8", text: "#854d0e" };
    36→    case "LOW":
    37→    default:
    38→      return { border: "#16a34a", bg: "#f0fdf4", text: "#166534" };
    39→  }
    40→}
    41→
    42→function safeHostname(url: string): string {
    43→  try {
    44→    return new URL(url).hostname.replace("www.", "");
    45→  } catch {
    46→    return url;
    47→  }
    48→}
    49→
    50→function getDownloadLabel(url: string): { label: string; isDirect: boolean } {
    51→  const lower = url.toLowerCase();
    52→  const filename = lower.split("/").pop() ?? "";
    53→
    54→  // Detect platform from common patterns in the URL or filename
    55→  const platforms: [RegExp, string][] = [
    56→    [/linux.*amd64|amd64.*linux/, "Linux x64"],
    57→    [/linux.*arm64|arm64.*linux|linux.*aarch64/, "Linux ARM64"],
    58→    [/linux.*386|linux.*i386/, "Linux x86"],
    59→    [/darwin.*arm64|arm64.*darwin|macos.*arm64|osx.*arm64/, "macOS ARM64"],
    60→    [/darwin.*amd64|amd64.*darwin|macos.*amd64|osx.*amd64/, "macOS x64"],
    61→    [/darwin|macos|osx/, "macOS"],
    62→    [/windows.*amd64|amd64.*windows|win64/, "Windows x64"],
    63→    [/windows.*386|win32/, "Windows x86"],
    64→    [/windows/, "Windows"],
    65→  ];
    66→
    67→  // Check if this is a direct binary (archive or executable)
    68→  const isArchive = /\.(tar\.gz|tar\.xz|zip|deb|rpm|dmg|msi|exe|pkg|appimage)(\?|$)/i.test(url);
    69→
    70→  if (isArchive) {
    71→    for (const [pattern, label] of platforms) {
    72→      if (pattern.test(filename) || pattern.test(url)) {
    73→        return { label, isDirect: true };
    74→      }
    75→    }
    76→    // Direct download but platform unknown — use filename
    77→    const cleanName = (url.split("/").pop() ?? "").split("?")[0];
    78→    return { label: cleanName || safeHostname(url), isDirect: true };
    79→  }
    80→
    81→  // Not a direct binary — generic link
    82→  return { label: safeHostname(url), isDirect: false };
    83→}
    84→
    85→export function SemanticReleaseDetail() {
    86→  // Read IDs from URL path — useParams() returns stale "0" in static export
    87→  const projectId = getPathSegment(1); // /projects/{id}/semantic-releases/{srId}
    88→  const srId = getPathSegment(3);
    89→  const router = useRouter();
    90→  const { data, isLoading } = useSWR(`sr-${srId}`, () => srApi.get(srId));
    91→  const { data: projectData } = useSWR(`project-${projectId}`, () =>
    92→    projectsApi.get(projectId),
    93→  );
    94→  const { data: srSourcesData } = useSWR(`sr-sources-${srId}`, () =>
    95→    srApi.getSources(srId),
    96→  );
    97→  const { data: sourcesData } = useSWR(`project-sources-${projectId}`, () =>
    98→    sourcesApi.listByProject(projectId),
    99→  );
   100→  const { data: contextSourcesData } = useSWR(
   101→    `project-context-sources-${projectId}`,

   104→
   105→  if (isLoading) {
   106→    return (

   109→          className="text-[13px] text-[#9ca3af]"

   111→        >
   112→          Loading...
   113→        </div>
   114→      </div>
   115→    );
   116→  }
   117→
   118→  const sr = data?.data;
   119→  if (!sr) {
   120→    return (

   122→        <div
   123→          className="text-[13px] text-[#9ca3af]"
   124→          style={{ fontFamily: "var(--font-dm-sans)" }}
   125→        >
   126→          Semantic release not found
   127→        </div>
   128→      </div>

   131→
   132→  const project = projectData?.data;

   136→  const contextSourcesList = contextSourcesData?.data ?? [];
   137→
   138→  const handleDelete = async () => {
   139→    if (!confirm("Delete this semantic release?")) return;
   140→    try {
   141→      await srApi.delete(srId);
   142→      router.push("/semantic-releases");
   143→    } catch {
   144→      alert("Failed to delete semantic release.");
   145→    }
   146→  };
   147→
   148→  const report = sr.report;
   149→  const riskLevel = report?.risk_level?.toUpperCase();

   157→    statusChecks.length > 0 ||
   158→    downloadLinks.length > 0 ||
   159→    downloadCommands.length > 0;
   160→
   161→  return (
   162→    <div className="fade-in mx-auto max-w-[760px]">
   163→      {/* 1. Back link */}
   164→      <Link
   165→        href="/semantic-releases"
   166→        className="mb-6 inline-flex items-center gap-1.5 transition-colors hover:opacity-70"
   167→        style={{
   168→          fontFamily: "var(--font-dm-sans)",
   169→          fontSize: "13px",
   170→          color: "#6b7280",
   171→        }}
   172→      >
   173→        <ArrowLeft size={14} />
   174→        Back to Semantic Releases
   175→      </Link>
   176→
   177→      {/* 2. Project byline */}
   178→      {project?.name && (
   179→        <p
   180→          className="mb-1 text-[13px] italic text-[#9ca3af]"

   182→        >
   183→          {project.name}

   185→      )}
   186→
   187→      {/* 3. Version heading */}
   188→      <h1
   189→        className="text-[42px] font-bold tracking-tight text-[#111113] leading-[1.1]"

   368→
   369→              {/* Download commands as copiable code */}
   370→              {downloadCommands.length > 0 && (
   371→                <div className="space-y-2">
   372→                  {downloadCommands.map((cmd) => (
   373→                    <div

   376→                      style={{
   377→                        backgroundColor: "#fafaf9",

   379→                      }}

   447→                      >

   449→                      </th>
   450→                      <th

   453→                      >

   455→                      </th>
   456→                      <th

   458→                        style={{ fontFamily: "var(--font-dm-sans)" }}
   459→                      >

   461→                      </th>
   462→                    </tr>
   463→                  </thead>
   464→                  <tbody>

   470→                          : source?.provider === "dockerhub"

   472→                            : source?.provider === "ecr-public"

   474→                              : null;

   484→                            ) : (

   488→                            )}
   489→                          </td>

   491→                            <span
   492→                              className="text-[13px] text-[#374151]"
   493→                              style={{

   495→                              }}
   496→                            >
   497→                              {source?.repository ?? "\u2014"}
   498→                            </span>
   499→                          </td>

   513→                              </a>

   522→                            >

   524→                            </span>

   527→                      );

   529→                  </tbody>
   530→                </table>

   533→          )}
   534→
   535→          {/* 12. Context Sources */}
   536→          {contextSourcesList.length > 0 && (

   577→                        </a>

Good, I now have a clear picture of the full system. Let me share what I've found and ask my first clarifying question.

## Current State Analysis

I've reviewed the full pipeline — the LLM prompt, the `SemanticReport` struct, notification formatting (Slack/Discord), and the frontend detail page. Here's what I see as the current report structure:

**Schema fields (10 total):**
1. `subject` — "Ready to Deploy: Geth v1.10.15 (Critical Update)"
2. `risk_level` — CRITICAL/HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW
3. `risk_reason` — paragraph explaining why
4. `status_checks` — ["Docker Image Verified", "Binaries Available"]
5. `changelog_summary` — one-line summary
6. `availability` — GA/RC/Beta
7. `adoption` — paragraph about network adoption
8. `urgency` — Critical/High/Medium/Low
9. `recommendation` — 1-2 sentence actionable advice
10. `download_commands` + `download_links` — install commands and URLs

**Problems I can see:**
- **`risk_level` and `urgency` are near-duplicates** — both are severity scales that usually say the same thing
- **`availability` is buried** — GA/RC/Beta is critical context but shown as a small field
- **`recommendation` exists but isn't shown** on the frontend detail page at all
- **`adoption` as a standalone section** feels like filler — it's usually vague ("12% of network updated")
- **`changelog_summary` is at the bottom** of the detail page despite being a key piece of info
- **The notification (Slack/Discord) dumps every field** with dividers between each, making it very long for what's often simple info
- **Subject line** duplicates project+version which is already in the header

> AGENT

Clear picture. Here are three approaches:

## Approach 1: Compact Card (Recommended)

Condense the notification to a single tight block — one header, a few key lines, and a link:

**Slack mockup:**
```
📦 Geth v1.10.15 — LOW RISK
Fixes sync bug in block 14,000,000  
docker pull ethereum/client-go:v1.10.15
View on GitHub  •  View in Changelogue
```

**What changes:**
- Header combines project + version + risk in one line (no separate "Subject")
- Changelog summary is the body (1 line)
- Download command inline
- Footer links only
- Drop: adoption, availability, status checks, recommendation, urgency (all available in full report via link)

**Pros:** Maximum signal-to-noise. Quick scan. Notifications become actionable alerts, not reports.
**Cons:** Important context (risk reason, status checks) only available by clicking through.

---

## Approach 2: Two-Tier Card

Keep a compact primary block but add risk reason as a single indented line when risk is HIGH or CRITICAL:

```
⚫ Geth v1.10.15 — CRITICAL RISK
Hard Fork detected — binaries must be deployed before block 18,000,000
Fixes sync bug in block 14,000,000
docker pull ethereum/client-go:v1.10.15
View on GitHub  •  View in Changelogue
```

For LOW/MEDIUM risk, same as Approach 1 (no risk reason line).

**Pros:** Critical alerts get the extra context they need. Low-risk stays compact.
**Cons:** Slightly more complex formatting logic.

---

## Approach 3: Keep All Fields, Visual Collapse

Keep the current data but use Slack's Block Kit / Discord embeds more aggressively — put risk+urgency+availability as inline fields in a single row, collapse adoption and status checks into the footer.

**Pros:** No information loss.
**Cons:** Still verbose. Doesn't solve the core problem — too many fields.

---

My recommendation is **Approach 2: Two-Tier Card**. It gives you the compact glanceability you want while ensuring critical risks still surface their reason directly in the notification. The full report is always one click away.

> AGENT

## Design: Two-Tier Compact Notifications

### Semantic Report Schema

No changes to the `SemanticReport` struct or the LLM prompt — the agent keeps producing all fields. The change is purely in how notifications format the data. The frontend detail page and webhook payload remain unchanged.

Does this part make sense — keeping the schema/prompt the same and only changing notification rendering?

### Slack Notification Format

**LOW / MEDIUM risk:**
```
📦 Geth v1.10.15 — 🟢 LOW RISK
Fixes sync bug in block 14,000,000
docker pull ethereum/client-go:v1.10.15
GitHub · View on GitHub  |  View in Changelogue
```

**HIGH / CRITICAL risk (adds risk reason):**
```
📦 Geth v1.10.15 — ⚫ CRITICAL RISK
⚠️ Hard Fork detected — binaries must be deployed before block 18,000,000
Fixes sync bug in block 14,000,000
docker pull ethereum/client-go:v1.10.15
GitHub · View on GitHub  |  View in Changelogue
```

**Slack Block Kit structure:**
1. **Header block** — `{emoji} {ProjectName} {Version} — {riskEmoji} {RISK_LEVEL} RISK`
2. **Section (conditional)** — Risk reason, only if risk is HIGH or CRITICAL
3. **Section** — Changelog summary (1 line)
4. **Section (conditional)** — First download command as code block (if any)
5. **Context block** — Provider + source link + Changelogue link

**Dropped from notifications:** subject, urgency, availability, adoption, recommendation, status checks, extra download commands/links. All remain available in the full report.

### Discord Notification Format

Same two-tier logic, using a Discord embed:

- **Title:** `{ProjectName} {Version}`
- **Description (compact):**
  ```
  ⚫ **CRITICAL RISK**
  Hard Fork detected — binaries must be deployed before block 18,000,000

  Fixes sync bug in block 14,000,000
  `docker pull ethereum/client-go:v1.10.15`

  [View on GitHub](url)  •  [View in Changelogue](url)
  ```
- **Color:** Risk-based (existing color scheme)
- **Fields:** Removed entirely (no more Risk Level / Urgency / Availability / Adoption inline fields)
- **Footer:** `GitHub · ethereum/go-ethereum` (existing)

For LOW/MEDIUM, the risk reason line is omitted.

### Webhook Payload

No changes — webhooks already send the full structured `SemanticReport` as JSON. Consumers format as they wish.

### Files Changed

1. `internal/routing/slack.go` — rewrite `buildSemanticBlocks()` 
2. `internal/routing/discord.go` — rewrite `buildSemanticEmbed()`
3. Corresponding test files if they exist

### What's NOT Changing

- `SemanticReport` struct
- LLM agent prompt (`DefaultInstruction`)
- Frontend detail page
- Webhook payload format
- API response format

> AGENT

That does mean we need a schema change after all — let me update the design to reflect this.

## Updated Design

### Schema Change

In `SemanticReport`:
- Remove `risk_level` and `risk_reason`
- Keep `urgency` as the primary severity field
- Add `urgency_reason` as the explanation
- Keep everything else

```go
type SemanticReport struct {
    Subject          string   `json:"subject"`
    Urgency          string   `json:"urgency"`          // Critical|High|Medium|Low
    UrgencyReason    string   `json:"urgency_reason"`   // Why this urgency level
    StatusChecks     []string `json:"status_checks"`
    ChangelogSummary string   `json:"changelog_summary"`
    DownloadCommands []string `json:"download_commands,omitempty"`
    DownloadLinks    []string `json:"download_links,omitempty"`
    Summary          string   `json:"summary,omitempty"`
    Availability     string   `json:"availability"`
    Adoption         string   `json:"adoption"`
    Recommendation   string   `json:"recommendation"`
    // Backward compat — old reports may still have these
    RiskLevel        string   `json:"risk_level,omitempty"`
    RiskReason       string   `json:"risk_reason,omitempty"`
}
```

### LLM Prompt Update

Replace `risk_level` and `risk_reason` with `urgency` and `urgency_reason` in `DefaultInstruction`.

### Notification Format (Slack)

**LOW/MEDIUM:**
```
📦 Geth v1.10.15 — 🟢 Low Urgency
Fixes sync bug in block 14,000,000
docker pull ethereum/client-go:v1.10.15
GitHub · View on GitHub  |  View in Changelogue
```

**CRITICAL/HIGH:**
```
📦 Geth v1.10.15 — ⚫ Critical Urgency
⚠️ Hard Fork detected — deploy before block 18,000,000
Fixes sync bug in block 14,000,000
docker pull ethereum/client-go:v1.10.15
GitHub · View on GitHub  |  View in Changelogue
```

### Notification Format (Discord)

Same logic, embed description, color derived from `urgency` instead of `risk_level`.

### Files Changed

1. `internal/models/semantic_release.go` — update `SemanticReport` struct
2. `internal/agent/orchestrator.go` — update `DefaultInstruction` prompt
3. `internal/routing/slack.go` — rewrite `buildSemanticBlocks()`, update `riskEmoji()` 
4. `internal/routing/discord.go` — rewrite `buildSemanticEmbed()`, update `discordRiskColor()`
5. `web/lib/api/types.ts` — update `SemanticReport` type
6. `web/components/semantic-releases/semantic-release-detail.tsx` — use `urgency`/`urgency_reason` instead of `risk_level`/`risk_reason`
7. Test files for any of the above

### Backward Compatibility

Old reports stored in DB still have `risk_level` and `risk_reason` in their JSONB. The struct keeps those fields as `omitempty` so they still deserialize. Display code falls back: `urgency || risk_level`, `urgency_reason || risk_reason`.

{
  "file_path": "/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/docs/plans/2026-03-03-compact-notifications-design.md",
  "content": "# Compact Two-Tier Notifications Design\n\n## Problem\n\nSemantic release notifications (Slack/Discord) are too verbose — they dump every report field (risk, urgency, availability, adoption, status checks, changelog, download commands, download links, recommendation) with dividers between each, making them hard to scan quickly.\n\nAdditionally, `risk_level` and `urgency` are near-duplicates that usually hold the same value.\n\n## Goals\n\n- Notifications should be glanceable (5-6 lines)\n- Surface: urgency level, what changed, download command\n- Add urgency reason detail only for Critical/High urgency\n- Consolidate `risk_level`/`risk_reason` into `urgency`/`urgency_reason`\n- Full details remain available via \"View in Changelogue\" link\n\n## Design\n\n### Schema Change\n\nReplace `risk_level` + `risk_reason` with `urgency_reason`. Keep `urgency` as the primary severity field.\n\n```go\ntype SemanticReport struct {\n    Subject          string   `json:\"subject\"`\n    Urgency          string   `json:\"urgency\"`          // Critical|High|Medium|Low\n    UrgencyReason    string   `json:\"urgency_reason\"`   // Why this urgency level\n    StatusChecks     []string `json:\"status_checks\"`\n    ChangelogSummary string   `json:\"changelog_summary\"`\n    DownloadCommands []string `json:\"download_commands,omitempty\"`\n    DownloadLinks    []string `json:\"download_links,omitempty\"`\n    Summary          string   `json:\"summary,omitempty\"`\n    Availability     string   `json:\"availability\"`\n    Adoption         string   `json:\"adoption\"`\n    Recommendation   string   `json:\"recommendation\"`\n    // Backward compat — old reports still have these\n    RiskLevel        string   `json:\"risk_level,omitempty\"`\n    RiskReason       string   `json:\"risk_reason,omitempty\"`\n}\n```\n\n### LLM Prompt Update\n\nReplace `risk_level` and `risk_reason` with `urgency` and `urgency_reason` in `DefaultInstruction`.\n\n### Slack Notification — Two-Tier Format\n\n**Low/Medium urgency (compact):**\n```\n📦 Geth v1.10.15 — 🟢 Low Urgency\nFixes sync bug in block 14,000,000\ndocker pull ethereum/client-go:v1.10.15\nGitHub · ethereum/go-ethereum  |  View on GitHub  |  View in Changelogue\n```\n\n**Critical/High urgency (adds reason):**\n```\n📦 Geth v1.10.15 — ⚫ Critical Urgency\n⚠️ Hard Fork detected — deploy before block 18,000,000\nFixes sync bug in block 14,000,000\ndocker pull ethereum/client-go:v1.10.15\nGitHub · ethereum/go-ethereum  |  View on GitHub  |  View in Changelogue\n```\n\nBlock Kit structure:\n1. Header — `{ProjectName} {Version} — {emoji} {Urgency} Urgency`\n2. Section (conditional, Critical/High only) — urgency reason\n3. Section — changelog summary\n4. Section (conditional) — first download command as code\n5. Context — provider info + links\n\n**Dropped from notifications:** subject, risk_level, availability, adoption, recommendation, status checks, extra download commands/links.\n\n### Discord Notification — Two-Tier Format\n\nSame logic using Discord embed:\n- Title: `{ProjectName} {Version}`\n- Description: urgency badge + optional reason + changelog + download command + links\n- Color: derived from urgency (Critical=near-black, High=red, Medium=amber, Low=green)\n- No inline fields (removes risk/urgency/availability/adoption fields)\n- Footer: provider + repository\n\n### Webhook Payload\n\nNo changes — continues sending full structured SemanticReport JSON.\n\n### Frontend Detail Page\n\nUpdate to use `urgency`/`urgency_reason` instead of `risk_level`/`risk_reason` with fallback to old fields for backward compatibility.\n\n### Backward Compatibility\n\nOld JSONB reports in the database still contain `risk_level` and `risk_reason`. The struct retains these as `omitempty`. Display code falls back: use `urgency` if present, else `risk_level`; use `urgency_reason` if present, else `risk_reason`.\n\n## Files Changed\n\n1. `internal/models/semantic_release.go` — update SemanticReport struct\n2. `internal/agent/orchestrator.go` — update DefaultInstruction prompt\n3. `internal/routing/slack.go` — rewrite buildSemanticBlocks()\n4. `internal/routing/discord.go` — rewrite buildSemanticEmbed(), discordRiskColor()\n5. `internal/routing/sender.go` — rename riskEmoji() to urgencyEmoji()\n6. `web/lib/api/types.ts` — update SemanticReport TypeScript type\n7. `web/components/semantic-releases/semantic-release-detail.tsx` — use urgency/urgency_reason with fallback\n8. Test files for affected packages\n\n## Not Changing\n\n- Database schema (JSONB is schema-less)\n- API response format (struct change is additive)\n- Webhook payload structure\n- Agent tools or sub-agents\n"
}
```

    15→func TestSlackSender_Send(t *testing.T) {
    16→	var received []byte
    17→	srv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
    18→		if r.Header.Get("Content-Type") != "application/json" {
    19→			t.Errorf("expected Content-Type application/json, got %s", r.Header.Get("Content-Type"))
    20→		}
    21→		received, _ = io.ReadAll(r.Body)
    22→		w.WriteHeader(http.StatusOK)
    23→	}))
    24→	defer srv.Close()
    25→
    26→	sender := &SlackSender{Client: srv.Client()}
    27→	ch := &models.NotificationChannel{
    28→		Type:   "slack",
    29→		Config: json.RawMessage(`{"webhook_url": "` + srv.URL + `"}`),
    30→	}
    31→	msg := Notification{
    32→		Title:   "New release: geth v1.14.0",
    33→		Body:    "Released on GitHub with security fixes",
    34→		Version: "v1.14.0",
    35→	}
    36→
    37→	err := sender.Send(context.Background(), ch, msg)
    38→	if err != nil {
    39→		t.Fatalf("unexpected error: %v", err)
    40→	}
    41→	if len(received) == 0 {
    42→		t.Fatal("expected Slack to receive payload")
    43→	}
    44→
    45→	// Verify it's a valid Slack Block Kit payload with blocks.
    46→	var payload map[string]interface{}
    47→	if err := json.Unmarshal(received, &payload); err != nil {
    48→		t.Fatalf("received invalid JSON: %v", err)
    49→	}
    50→	blocks, ok := payload["blocks"]
    51→	if !ok {
    52→		t.Fatal("expected 'blocks' key in Slack payload")
    53→	}
    54→	blockList, ok := blocks.([]interface{})
    55→	if !ok || len(blockList) < 2 {
    56→		t.Fatalf("expected at least 2 blocks, got %v", blocks)
    57→	}
    58→}

    91→func TestSlackSender_SemanticReport(t *testing.T) {
    92→	var received []byte
    93→	srv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
    94→		received, _ = io.ReadAll(r.Body)

    97→	defer srv.Close()
    98→
    99→	sender := &SlackSender{Client: srv.Client()}
   100→	ch := &models.NotificationChannel{
   101→		Type:   "slack",
   102→		Config: json.RawMessage(`{"webhook_url": "` + srv.URL + `"}`),
   103→	}
   104→
   105→	reportJSON := `{
   106→		"subject": "Ready to Deploy: go-ethereum v1.16.4",
   107→		"risk_level": "HIGH",
   108→		"risk_reason": "Version not found in sources",
   109→		"status_checks": ["Binaries Unverified", "Docker Image Unverified"],
   110→		"changelog_summary": "Security fixes and performance improvements",
   111→		"availability": "GA",
   112→		"adoption": "Recommended for production",
   113→		"urgency": "High",
   114→		"recommendation": "Deploy after verifying checksums",
   115→		"download_commands": ["docker pull ethereum/client-go:v1.16.4"],
   116→		"download_links": ["https://github.com/ethereum/go-ethereum/releases/tag/v1.16.4"]
   117→	}`
   118→
   119→	msg := Notification{
   120→		Title:   "Semantic Release Report: go-ethereum v1.16.4",
   121→		Body:    reportJSON,
   122→		Version: "v1.16.4",
   123→	}
   124→
   125→	err := sender.Send(context.Background(), ch, msg)
   126→	if err != nil {
   127→		t.Fatalf("unexpected error: %v", err)
   128→	}
   129→
   130→	var payload slackPayload
   131→	if err := json.Unmarshal(received, &payload); err != nil {
   132→		t.Fatalf("received invalid JSON: %v", err)
   133→	}
   134→
   135→	// Should have many more blocks than the simple 2-block fallback
   136→	if len(payload.Blocks) < 6 {
   137→		t.Fatalf("expected at least 6 blocks for semantic report, got %d", len(payload.Blocks))
   138→	}
   139→
   140→	// First block should be header
   141→	if payload.Blocks[0].Type != "header" {
   142→		t.Fatalf("expected first block to be header, got %s", payload.Blocks[0].Type)
   143→	}
   144→
   145→	// Second block should contain the subject
   146→	if payload.Blocks[1].Text == nil || !strings.Contains(payload.Blocks[1].Text.Text, "Ready to Deploy") {
   147→		t.Fatal("expected second block to contain the subject")
   148→	}
   149→
   150→	// Should have a section with fields (risk, urgency, availability)
   151→	hasFields := false
   152→	for _, b := range payload.Blocks {
   153→		if len(b.Fields) > 0 {
   154→			hasFields = true
   155→			// Check risk emoji is present
   156→			found := false
   157→			for _, f := range b.Fields {
   158→				if strings.Contains(f.Text, "🔴") && strings.Contains(f.Text, "HIGH") {
   159→					found = true
   160→				}
   161→			}
   162→			if !found {
   163→				t.Fatal("expected risk field with 🔴 HIGH emoji")
   164→			}
   165→		}
   166→	}
   167→	if !hasFields {
   168→		t.Fatal("expected at least one section with fields")
   169→	}
   170→
   171→	// Should have download commands in a code block
   172→	hasCode := false
   173→	for _, b := range payload.Blocks {
   174→		if b.Text != nil && strings.Contains(b.Text.Text, "```") && strings.Contains(b.Text.Text, "docker pull") {
   175→			hasCode = true
   176→		}
   177→	}
   178→	if !hasCode {
   179→		t.Fatal("expected download commands in a code block")
   180→	}
   181→
   182→	// Should have context footer
   183→	lastBlock := payload.Blocks[len(payload.Blocks)-1]
   184→	if lastBlock.Type != "context" {
   185→		t.Fatalf("expected last block to be context, got %s", lastBlock.Type)
   186→	}
   187→}
   188→
   189→func TestSlackSender_NonReportFallback(t *testing.T) {
   190→	var received []byte
   191→	srv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
   192→		received, _ = io.ReadAll(r.Body)
   193→		w.WriteHeader(http.StatusOK)
   194→	}))
   195→	defer srv.Close()
   196→
   197→	sender := &SlackSender{Client: srv.Client()}
   198→	ch := &models.NotificationChannel{
   199→		Type:   "slack",
   200→		Config: json.RawMessage(`{"webhook_url": "` + srv.URL + `"}`),
   201→	}
   202→
   203→	// Plain text body (not a semantic report) should use simple fallback
   204→	msg := Notification{
   205→		Title:   "New release: geth v1.14.0",
   206→		Body:    "Released on GitHub with security fixes",
   207→		Version: "v1.14.0",
   208→	}
   209→
   210→	err := sender.Send(context.Background(), ch, msg)
   211→	if err != nil {
   212→		t.Fatalf("unexpected error: %v", err)
   213→	}
   214→
   215→	var payload slackPayload
   216→	if err := json.Unmarshal(received, &payload); err != nil {
   217→		t.Fatalf("received invalid JSON: %v", err)
   218→	}
   219→
   220→	// Fallback should produce exactly 2 blocks (header + section)
   221→	if len(payload.Blocks) != 2 {
   222→		t.Fatalf("expected 2 blocks for non-report, got %d", len(payload.Blocks))
   223→	}
   224→	if payload.Blocks[0].Type != "header" {
   225→		t.Fatalf("expected header block, got %s", payload.Blocks[0].Type)
   226→	}
   227→	if payload.Blocks[1].Type != "section" {
   228→		t.Fatalf("expected section block, got %s", payload.Blocks[1].Type)
   229→	}
   230→}
   231→
   232→func TestSlackSender_RawJSONFallback(t *testing.T) {
   233→	var received []byte
   234→	srv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
   235→		received, _ = io.ReadAll(r.Body)
   236→		w.WriteHeader(http.StatusOK)
   237→	}))
   238→	defer srv.Close()
   239→
   240→	sender := &SlackSender{Client: srv.Client()}
   241→	ch := &models.NotificationChannel{
   242→		Type:   "slack",
   243→		Config: json.RawMessage(`{"webhook_url": "` + srv.URL + `"}`),
   244→	}
   245→
   246→	// Raw release JSON with metadata on the Notification struct (as worker now provides)
   247→	msg := Notification{
   248→		Title:       "zkSync Era — New release: zkos-0.29.4-rc1",
   249→		Body:        `{"changelog":"Fixed wrong genesis commit","prerelease":"false","release_url":"https://github.com/matter-labs/zksync-era/releases/tag/zkos-0.29.4-rc1"}`,
   250→		Version:     "zkos-0.29.4-rc1",
   251→		ProjectName: "zkSync Era",
   252→		Provider:    "github",
   253→		Repository:  "matter-labs/zksync-era",
   254→		SourceURL:   "https://github.com/matter-labs/zksync-era/releases/tag/zkos-0.29.4-rc1",
   255→		ReleaseURL:  "https://changelogue.example.com/releases/rel-1",
   256→	}
   257→
   258→	err := sender.Send(context.Background(), ch, msg)
   259→	if err != nil {
   260→		t.Fatalf("unexpected error: %v", err)
   261→	}
   262→
   263→	var payload slackPayload
   264→	if err := json.Unmarshal(received, &payload); err != nil {
   265→		t.Fatalf("received invalid JSON: %v", err)
   266→	}
   267→
   268→	// Should have header + changelog section + context with links = 3 blocks
   269→	if len(payload.Blocks) != 3 {
   270→		t.Fatalf("expected 3 blocks, got %d", len(payload.Blocks))
   271→	}
   272→
   273→	// Changelog text should appear without raw JSON
   274→	changelogBlock := payload.Blocks[1]
   275→	if changelogBlock.Text == nil || !strings.Contains(changelogBlock.Text.Text, "Fixed wrong genesis commit") {
   276→		t.Fatal("expected changelog text in second block")
   277→	}
   278→	if strings.Contains(changelogBlock.Text.Text, "release_url") {
   279→		t.Fatal("raw JSON keys should not appear in formatted output")
   280→	}
   281→
   282→	// Context block should have source info and links
   283→	contextBlock := payload.Blocks[2]
   284→	if contextBlock.Type != "context" {
   285→		t.Fatalf("expected context block, got %s", contextBlock.Type)
   286→	}
   287→	if len(contextBlock.Elements) == 0 {
   288→		t.Fatal("expected context elements")
   289→	}
   290→	contextText := contextBlock.Elements[0].Text
   291→	if !strings.Contains(contextText, "View on GitHub") {
   292→		t.Fatal("expected 'View on GitHub' link in context")
   293→	}
   294→	if !strings.Contains(contextText, "View in Changelogue") {
   295→		t.Fatal("expected 'View in Changelogue' link in context")
   296→	}
   297→	if !strings.Contains(contextText, "matter-labs/zksync-era") {
   298→		t.Fatal("expected repository name in context")
   299→	}
   300→}

    21→	}

    45→	if len(decoded.DownloadLinks) != 1 {
    46→		t.Errorf("download_links: got %d items, want 1", len(decoded.DownloadLinks))

    48→}

    14→func TestDiscordSender_Send(t *testing.T) {
    15→	var received []byte
    16→	srv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
    17→		if r.Header.Get("Content-Type") != "application/json" {
    18→			t.Errorf("expected Content-Type application/json, got %s", r.Header.Get("Content-Type"))
    19→		}
    20→		received, _ = io.ReadAll(r.Body)
    21→		w.WriteHeader(http.StatusNoContent)
    22→	}))
    23→	defer srv.Close()
    24→
    25→	sender := &DiscordSender{Client: srv.Client()}
    26→	ch := &models.NotificationChannel{
    27→		Type:   "discord",
    28→		Config: json.RawMessage(`{"webhook_url": "` + srv.URL + `"}`),
    29→	}
    30→	msg := Notification{
    31→		Title:   "New release: geth v1.14.0",
    32→		Body:    "Released on GitHub with security fixes",
    33→		Version: "v1.14.0",
    34→	}
    35→
    36→	err := sender.Send(context.Background(), ch, msg)
    37→	if err != nil {
    38→		t.Fatalf("unexpected error: %v", err)
    39→	}
    40→	if len(received) == 0 {
    41→		t.Fatal("expected Discord to receive payload")
    42→	}
    43→
    44→	// Verify it's a valid Discord webhook payload with embeds.
    45→	var payload map[string]interface{}
    46→	if err := json.Unmarshal(received, &payload); err != nil {
    47→		t.Fatalf("received invalid JSON: %v", err)
    48→	}
    49→	embeds, ok := payload["embeds"]
    50→	if !ok {
    51→		t.Fatal("expected 'embeds' key in Discord payload")
    52→	}
    53→	embedList, ok := embeds.([]interface{})
    54→	if !ok || len(embedList) < 1 {
    55→		t.Fatalf("expected at least 1 embed, got %v", embeds)
    56→	}
    57→
    58→	// Verify the embed contains our title.
    59→	embed := embedList[0].(map[string]interface{})
    60→	if title, ok := embed["title"].(string); !ok || title != msg.Title {
    61→		t.Errorf("expected embed title %q, got %q", msg.Title, title)
    62→	}
    63→}
    64→
